                                     Appendix D

         Table B—Basic Skills Engagements

    Basic skills engagements, called Table B, are designed to assist the
    commander by providing additional on-demand training for those crews
    having difficulty obtaining appropriate reticle aim prior to advancing to
    Table V. These engagements are built as a training stepping-stone to Table
    V for crews experiencing difficulty on specific live-fire capabilities tasks.
    The basic skills engagements are a menu-based, commander-selected
    engagements to incorporate into the unit training plan. They augment the
    engagements on Table IV when retraining on failed tasks will not best
    prepare the crew for advancement to Table V, Practice, or utilization of a
    simulator system are unavailable.

    These engagements are focused on the six capabilities-based tasks for the
    Abrams-series tank; tank commander, machine gun, main gun, change of
    weapon system or ammunition, degraded skills, or simultaneous
    engagements. Commanders select any engagement for integration into their
    training plan based on available resources (time and training ammunition)
    to provide experience to struggling crews prior to Table V, Practice.

OVERVIEW OF TABLE B

     D-1. Table B provides crews the ability to retrain on specific capability-based skills in
     areas where they are experiencing difficulties. Rather than refire failed engagements on
     Table IV, Basic, the commander can select an appropriate Table B engagement that
     builds additional rigor and skills during retraining opportunities.

     D-2. These engagements are not resourced. They are provided as a training tool for
     commanders to conduct retraining and cross-training opportunities with additional
     variety and increased rigor to best prepare crews for Table V.

     D-3. The planning and preparation process—including the uniform, facilities, targetry,
     and range support requirements—are the same as Table IV, Basic. Refer to chapter 4 for
     detailed information for those processes.

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120  399

                                      Appendix E

                  Complex Engagements

    Table C, Complex Engagements, is a course of fire developed for
    commanders to challenge their best crews. Table C serves as a live-fire
    check on mastery skills on an Abrams platform. It is intended for use after
    a crew has successfully qualified on their tank within the past six months.
    The engagements were specifically designed by Master Gunners to focus on
    the capabilities of the Abrams tank, its ammunition, and fire control system
    functionality. It should only be used for crews that have demonstrated an
    exceptional level of skill with their tank.
    This event is scored in the same manner as other engagements in this
    publication. Units electing to execute this course of fire will require detailed
    coordination with their supporting range operations branch to build a safe
    and effective scenario.

OVERVIEW OF TABLE C

     E-1. Table C, Complex Engagements, is fired from the crew’s vehicle and uses full
     caliber training ammunition on an authorized live-fire facility or safety certified training
     area. The training ammunition required to conduct Table C are not resourced. The unit
     may use training munitions available in their account or coordinate with their
     ammunition management office for additional resources, as available.

     E-2. Table C trains and evaluate the experienced crew’s ability to engage stationary or
     moving targets placed in a tactical array, during day and limited visibility conditions,
     with an operational or simulated degraded fire control system, as applicable. Crews
     execute these tasks from the offense and defense using full-caliber ammunition.

     E-3. This table requires each crew to successfully complete the live-fire prerequisites,
     all screening and zeroing procedures for their platform, successfully complete Table VI,
     Qualification, and are selected by the commander to conduct this event.

                            Experienced Crews Only

          Crews must be selected by the battalion commander or above to
          conduct this event. This table is designed to train and evaluate
          complex engagement skills, day and night, while fighting from the
          defense and during offensive engagements.

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120  413


---

Appendix E

PURPOSE OF TABLE C

     E-4. Table C provides a series of live-fire engagements to showcase a unit’s
     experienced crews on an Abrams tank, demonstrate their master of their profession, and
     to demonstrate their lethality in a rigorous, competitive course of fire.

METHOD—TABLE C

     E-5. Table C is a performance-based evaluation of advanced gunnery skills in a live
     environment under live-fire conditions. Conduct of Table C is from the crew’s tank
     using full-caliber ammunition and BESs. It utilizes the unit’s most experienced
     evaluators to educate, train, test, and retrain Soldiers to meet or exceed those standards.

END STATE—TABLE C

     E-6. The crew demonstrates mastery of their tank across a series of demanding
     engagements. The crews are challenged by introducing them to mastery-level
     engagements designed by Master Gunners based on large scale combat operations threat
     dilemmas. The unit receives an exceptionally well-trained crew that trains others on their
     knowledge and experiences gained.

TASK, CONDITIONS, AND STANDARD—TABLE C

     E-7. Outlined in figure E-1 are the task, conditions, and standard for Table VI,
     Qualification. Units may not add to the live-fire requirements or tasks associate with
     Table VI. The division commander or equivalent maintains the qualification course of
     fire standards and approved scenarios.

Figure E-1. Table C, Complex Engagements, task, conditions, and standard

414  TC 3-20.31-120  31 July 2025

                                                                                          Complex Engagements

PLAN FOR TABLE C

     E-8. Table C, Complex Engagements, is a live-fire training environment event using
     full-caliber and small arms training ammunition, BESs, and specific TADSS. This
     course of fire requires experienced VCEs and coordination with the higher headquarters
     for support.

                Use Experienced Vehicle Crew Evaluators

          Units should consider having experienced Master Gunners serve
          as VCEs for these engagements due to their inherent complexity.

     E-9. This section details the resources needed for planning purposes. It includes
     general information on the following topics for consideration during the planning
     process, which are—

          • Uniform—the minimum requirements for the combat vehicle crewmember’s
                fire-retardant clothing and protective equipment.

          • Facilities—available facilities that support the training event.
          • Training plan—the minimum tasks, general guidelines, and special instructions

                for the training event.
          • Time and throughput—the estimated time required to complete the training

                event including throughput considerations for planning.
          • Ammunition—any training munitions required to complete the training event.
          • TADSS—any available training aids that directly support or augment the

                training event.

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120  415

commanders to build future unit training plans.

offensive and retrograde engagements.

                              Complex Engagements

TRAINING PLAN FOR TABLE C

     E-14. The design of Table C is purposefully challenging to the crews while providing
     realistic engagements that exercise the crew’s ability to utilize the key capabilities of the
     vehicle’s fire control system. Units retrain as necessary with full-caliber ammunition
     using first-round hit savings (harvested ammunition) from the previous tables in the
     training strategy.

     E-15. All crew platform tables are multiple qualification criteria tables. Crews must
     score at least 70 points per target according to the target kill standards in TC 3-20.31 on
     single and multiple target engagements to qualify the engagement. Crews must also
     score more than 700 points overall with a minimum of 70 points per target during 7 of
     10 engagements on the qualification table to be used. Crews must qualify at least one
     engagement during day and one engagement during limited visibility to be rated as the
     top gun.

     E-16. Table C uses full caliber training ammunition on an authorized live-fire facility or
     safety certified training area that assesses an element’s tactical employment proficiency
     and lethality. Units are required to use full-scale targets at full range for the engagement
     scenarios unless specifically directed.

Scenario Requirements for Table C

     E-17. All engagements are evaluated using certified VCEs using DA Form 8265 and
     DA Form 8265-1 as the official record of training. Master Gunners must ensure the
     training records are included in each crew’s gunnery packet (see TC 3-20.31).

     E-18. The engagements listings are by capability type using the standard engagement
     naming structure listed in TC 3-20.31. It provides the requirements for each engagement,
     which are described in paragraphs E-19 through E-28.

Task Description—Table C

     E-19. The task description includes both the engagement number (the table number plus
     engagement task) followed by the system capability trained.

Firing Vehicle Posture—Table C

     E-20. The firing vehicle posture establishes the offense or defense condition for the
     engagement.

Firer—Table C

     E-21. The firer column identifies which crewmember is to engage the target listed.
     Targets defeated by a different crewmember listed as the firer result in an auto-zero
     penalty, failing to adhere to course conditions.

Weapon (or Ammunition)—Table C

     E-22. Identifies the weapon (coaxial, caliber .50, or primary armament ammunition
     type) that is used to defeat the threat. Design of each engagement is for a specific weapon
     or ammunition type (primary armament) for use, which relates to the crew conduct of

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120  419


---

Appendix E

     fire, optic selection, engagement techniques, or other unique implied tasks. Crews that
     fail to announce the appropriate weapon or ammunition, or appropriate selection for
     subsequent or supplemental engagements may be penalized IAW TC 3-20.31.

Target Type—Table C

     E-23. The target required for the presentation, IAW TC 25-8.

Target Posture—Table C

     E-24. The target posture establishes if the target is stationary, moving, or evasive for the
     engagement. See TC 3-20.31 for more information on target postures.

Range (to Target)—Table C

     E-25. The required range (full scale or equivalent scaled range) from the firing vehicle
     to the target. For offensive engagements, this distance calculation is from the center of
     the movement box to the target. The range to target distance is provided in meters and
     is displayed as a whole number, such as 600.

     E-26. The target may be placed at the distance indicated, plus or minus 200 meters. For
     example, when the range to target is listed as 600 meters, the target may be placed
     between 400 meters and 800 meters. This allows flexibility designing the scenario on
     varying maneuver range facilities with different capabilities, target counts, emplacement
     locations, and terrain restrictions.

     E-27. No change to the EM is authorized. The range bands listed with their applied
     tolerances do not require any adjustment to the EM. Units are not authorized to exceed
     the target placement tolerances.

Engagement Modifier—Table C

     E-28. The EM is the time in seconds required to achieve 100 points against the listed
     threat. To defeat the threat successfully, the crew must achieve the kill standard against
     the target and have an overall engagement time of no more than the EM plus 20 seconds.
     See TC 3-20.31 for the evaluation, timing, and scoring procedures.

                           No Deviations Authorized

          Units are not authorized to deviate from the specified target type,
          posture, range band, firing vehicle posture, or ammunition type.
          This maintains the standards for the complex engagements across
          the force.

420  TC 3-20.31-120  31 July 2025

Appendix E

TADSS—TABLE C

     E-32. This course requires the use of the CMU. The CMU is available on the digital
     range facility scheduled (if equipped). Coordination with the scheduled range facility
     manager will determine unit responsibilities for installation, troubleshooting,
     maintenance, and use of the devices ahead of the training day(s). If the range does not
     have a CMU available, unit may draw the CMUR from the TADSS warehouse. The
     CMUR differs from the CMU in that it stores all run data on a solid-state drive on the
     vehicle, for use with the provided AAR suite in the CMUR kit.

     E-33. The brigade commander must approve authorization for deviation from this
     requirement if TADSS are unavailable. Units should focus on training and reinforcing
     the skills in the direct fire kill chain on this table specifically with their thermal optics,
     day and night to best prepare them for the follow-on training events. Figure E-8 shows
     the TADSS required or recommended for Table C.

                                 Complex Engagements

PREPARE FOR TABLE C

E-34. Units must coordinate for the appropriate training facility, equipment, targetry,
training munitions, TADSS, and pyrotechnics to support Table C. During the
preparation phase, the unit should ensure the proofing team, VCEs, and other personnel
receive training to standard to support their role as evaluators and trainers. This includes
range officers in charge, range safety officers, and ammunition handlers.

TRAIN THE TRAINERS—TABLE C

E-35. Typically, units have certified each VC and gunner as VCEs prior entering the
live-fire density. Units must have certified evaluators to ensure the training objectives
for the training table are set and to provide effective AARs to each crew.

E-36. Additional information on leader certification and train the trainer actions is found
in TC 3-20.31. Units should review and plan for the appropriate training and
certifications well in advance of the training event.

VEHICLE CREW EVALUATORS—TABLE C

E-37. A trained and certified VCE team must be available to support prior to live fire to
evaluate a gunnery density correctly. After certification, the unit must ensure sufficient
VCEs are available to support the live-fire event. Evaluations of crew gunnery always
come from outside the firing platoon element, and for qualification purposes, VCEs
external to the battalion are required.

E-38. Having the correct number and type of evaluators per firing range maximizes the
unit’s throughput of firing vehicles on the range, reduce range downtime, and provide
for the highest quality evaluations. See TC 3-20.31 for more in-depth information on
VCEs, the evaluation team, and the evaluation process.

E-39.   To prepare the trainers, units provide—
     •     Commander training objectives.
     •     Conduct of the range briefing for the training event.
     •     Scenario and lane concept with crew tasks for training.
     •     Risk assessments.
     •     Identification of supporting doctrinal publications for use as primary
           references.
     •     TADSS, pyrotechnics, blanks, and BESs used during the training event (see
           appendix A for the training munitions requirements for the complete training
           strategy).

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120     425


---

Appendix E

RECONNAISSANCE OF THE SITE—TABLE C

     E-40. Commanders use an authorized training facility for Table C. Commanders follow
     the installation’s policies and procedures for formally scheduling the training location
     as far in advance as practical. See TC 3-20.31 for more in-depth information concerning
     range operations at the unit level.

     E-41. Once scheduled, the commander, Master Gunner, platoon leaders and platoon
     sergeants conduct a reconnaissance of the training areas to—

          • Verify sufficient space is available to support the entire unit through multiple
                training lanes.

          • Ensure multiple iterations or rotations through the scenario are achievable with
                appropriate retraining opportunities.

          • Ensure sufficient administrative areas are available to support group
                discussions, briefings, and AARs.

          • Provide sand table or terrain board.
          • Identify the ammunition supply point.
          • Determine the conduct of the range or the flow of training to maximize the

                live-fire range time.
          • Identify the range operating system and verify the training scenarios have been

                properly loaded.
          • Schedule proofing of the range, based on the higher headquarters OPORD.
          • Ensure requested TADSS are available if provided by the range facility.

ISSUE THE ORDER—TABLE C

     E-42. Commanders can issue an OPORD, WARNORD, or use the training schedule as
     necessary. The WARNORD should include the appropriate tasks, required leaders, and
     equipment needed, as well as the unit’s training timeline including walk-through,
     rehearsals, and AARs. WARNORDs for external organizations must include sufficient
     reaction time for the appropriate personnel and equipment needed.

426  TC 3-20.31-120  31 July 2025


---

                                  Complex Engagements

REHEARSE—TABLE C

E-43. The unit uses all training resources, materials, and equipment available to conduct
rehearsals of training events. During the rehearsal, commanders ensure their unit has
sufficient time to train to the specified standard including AARs and retraining time.
Commanders ensure their unit verifies optional scenarios that increase complexity when
warranted.

E-44.   Units should rehearse the training event from start to finish, and confirm—
     •     The VCEs understand the training scenarios and tasks.
     •     Sufficient space is available to provide appropriate distances for engagements
           ranges and target dispersion.
     •     Analog and digital systems are operational within the organization and to the
           higher headquarters regardless of their location.
     •     Additional crew training packages [DA Form 8265, DA Form 8265-1, and
           appropriate CCFs(zero) documentation] are complete and provide for multiple
     •     iterations.
     •     Sufficient administrative areas are available.
           Review AAR capabilities. Use of thru-sight video systems is encouraged to
     •     increase the fidelity and quality of the AARs.
     •     Location of retraining for crews as necessary.
           Accessibility to crew simulator systems, plumb and synchronization ramps,
           T&MBs, and additional bore sighting locations are available.

E-45. Once the unit completes the walk-through and training rehearsal, the commander
makes any necessary adjustments to the plan and issues FRAGORDs as necessary.

                     Proofing the Scenario

The proofing of the scenario is a demanding process that requires
a minimum of one training day. It should include all targetry, BESs,
and sequencing of the approved scenario. Proofing must be
completed from the firing vehicle type using the optics described in
the conditions of the firing task.

E-46. The Master Gunner is required to proof the scenario from a tank using the
appropriate optics from the established firing positions and firing boxes from the
approved scenario. This is a time-consuming task that will require one day to complete
to standard. All targets must be 90-percent visible through the optic directed by the
conditions of each task. For offensive or retrograde engagements, the targets must be
90-percent visible through 90-percent of the maneuver box, minimum.

31 July 2025      TC 3-20.31-120                                      427


---

Appendix E

EXECUTE—TABLE C, COMPLEX ENGAGEMENTS

     E-47. This section provides a basic overview of the engagements of Table C during
     execution. Units should refer to TC 3-20.31 for comprehensive information on
     establishing the range, opening instructions, hot and cold times, and other actions
     necessary to conduct live-fire training.

     E-48. Once the unit arrives at the training location, they should—
          • Conduct a safety briefing for the training event.
          • Provide a conduct of the range briefing to establish the sequence of events for
                the training day.
          • Conduct sensitive item checks prior to executing training.
          • Receive briefing from the VCE team.
          • Establish the firing order.
          • Identify the maintenance support area for any fire control or weapons issues on
                the range.
          • Install, align, and place TADSS into operation, as required.

     E-49. The training unit conducts informal AARs of each crew completing a phase of
     training within the training mission. During training, the commander, executive officer,
     1SG, and Master Gunner review the crew’s performance. They follow the unit SOP,
     prescribed tactics, techniques, procedures, and the appropriate T&EOs, and review
     completed crew score sheets and roll-up documentation for clarity and completeness.

             Note. The sequence of the engagements, the number of engagements during
             the day and night phases, and which engagements are fired in a chemical
             environment are determined by the evaluation commander.

ENGAGEMENT C1

     E-55. Engagement C1 is a retrograde engagement for the gunner using the coaxial
     machine gun. While on the offense, returning up-range with the main armament over

     figure E-12.

              Figure E-12. Engagement C1 overview

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120                       433


---

Appendix E

INDUCED ENGAGEMENT COMPLEXITY—C1

     E-56. A retrograde engagement with the vehicle engaging targets with the main
     armament over the back deck presents several challenges. This engagement replicates a
     tank moving from one battle position to another when the contact occurs.

     E-57. The tank crew is presented with several complex issues that require advanced
     understanding. This engagement introduces the crew to the following advanced topics:

          • Firing inhibits (back deck clearance switch).
          • Advanced target detection (fleeting threats).
          • Advanced machine gun engagement techniques.
          • Engaging threats using wide field of view.
          • Obscuration mitigation.
          • Driver movement techniques.

     E-58. The unit’s Master Gunner should leverage the Sabot Academy training materials
     available on these topics and discussions for the crew, particularly on advanced
     engagement techniques. These are available from the Sabot Academy teams site listed
     in the references at the end of this publication.

     E-59.   The Master Gunner must pay particular attention to:
          •
          •     Target visibility and thermal signature presented to the firing crew.
          •
          •     Synchronizing the third target presentation using lift lines in the range
                operating software suite.
          •     Adjustment of the delay time from 25 seconds to reflect the synchronized
          •     presentation time of the third target for evaluation purposes.
          •
                Educating the VCEs on the fluid nature of synchronized target evaluation
                (presentation time is based on firing vehicle proximity to the third target during
                movement and adjusts the standard 25 second delay).

                Slow tank movement results in the third target presented behind the crew
                expending exposure time.

                Appropriate tactical movement results in the third target presentation where the
                crew can acquire and engage rapidly.

                Aggressive tactical movement results in the third target presented earlier than
                expected.
                 The movement may occur where the first two threats are still active.
                 It results in a much greater engagement time for one or two targets in the

                     array.

434          TC 3-20.31-120       31 July 2025

     E-63. Typically, crews misunderstand how to use the defensive firing position to their
     benefit. This engagement reinforces the need of the crew to understand and master—

          • Machine gun mounting.
          • Smoke box fitment.

     Figure E-19. Engagement C3 overview

INDUCED ENGAGEMENT COMPLEXITY—C3

     E-70. This engagement presents the crew with several complex issues that require
     advanced understanding. This engagement introduces the crew to the following
     advanced topics:

          • Fire control system capabilities.
          • Lead/angle sensor.
          • Application of automatic lead.
          • Manual lead application.
          • Battlesight gunnery skills.
          • Target detection, recognition, and acquisition to the flank.
          • Ammunition.

                 Ballistic performance.
                 Exterior ballistics.
                 Standard lead adjustments.

     E-71. Ballistically, the MPAT round was selected for this engagement to emphasize the
     flight of a slower main gun ammunition type against extended range moving threats. For
     the unit, having crews observe the conduct of this engagement is a training opportunity
     on ballistics, fire control system capabilities, automatic lead, and other advanced topics.

     E-72. The unit’s Master Gunner should leverage the Sabot Academy training materials
     available on these topics and discussions for the crew. These are available from the Sabot
     Academy teams site listed in the references at the end of this publication.

     E-73. The Master Gunner must consider the route approved for the flanking movement
     of this offensive engagement. Proofing of the course road and targetry includes line-of-
     sight issues where from the gunner’s auxiliary sight, a berm, firing position, or other
     structure may obstruct the flight of the round that is unobservable through the primary
     sight.

                             VERIFY LINE OF SIGHT

                    The Master Gunner proofing the range must
                    verify there are no gun-target line obstructions
                    that cannot be observed through the primary
                    optic. Failing to verify the gun-target line
                    through the entire maneuver box against all
                    targets may cause a main gun round strike on
                    range infrastructure.

Appendix E

ENGAGEMENT C4

     E-76. Engagement C4 is an offensive main gun engagement for the gunner against
     multiple moving tanks (see figure E-22). Engagement C4 emphasizes speed, audacity,
     reloading skills, accuracy, and lethality; and is often referred to as the killer tank
     scenario.

                      Figure E-22. Engagement C4 overview

                           Four Moving Targets

     Units must ensure the range facility supports four moving targets
     from a firing box. Where range bands cannot be met, the unit will
     use moving targets at the ranges available and capable of safe
     execution.

446  TC 3-20.31-120  31 July 2025


---

                                  Complex Engagements

INDUCED ENGAGEMENT COMPLEXITY—C4

E-77. This engagement reinforces the need for a MRS update before and after the
engagement. This engagement introduces the crew to the following advanced topics:

     • Error budget.
     • Ballistics.
     • MRS subsystem.
     • Plumb and synchronization (lead in elevation).
     • Gun tube technology.

E-78. The unit’s Master Gunner should leverage the Sabot Academy training materials
available on these topics and discussions for the tank commander. These are available
from the Sabot Academy teams site listed in the references at the end of this publication.

E-79.   The Master Gunner must consider—
     •     The difficulty in accommodating the use of four moving targets during one
           engagement on some maneuver facilities.
     •     The necessity of alignment of moving target speed with the presentation time
           requirements and the mover track length.
     •     The criticality of thermalization of the moving targets, particularly when
           moving through varying terrain.
     •     Early proofing of the range correctly to identify any line-of-sight issues.
     •     The requirement to present the initial two moving targets on opposing sides of
           the range to ensure they are not viewable together in high magnification (10x).
     •     The desire to delay moving targets on opposing sides of the range (as well as
           presentation of targets moving outward to opposite sides of the range, pending
           range facility support of side-by-side presentation of the delay targets).

                 Delay Moving Presentation

The delay presentation may be presented from the center of the

range and move outward to the edges of the range. Units may alter

the presentation sequence based on the facility capabilities.

INDUCED ENGAGEMENT COMPLEXITY—C5

     E-83. Success on this engagement requires the crew to have exceptional crew
     coordination, conduct of fire, crew responses, reloading, and engagement skills. The
     complexity of the engagement requires the crew to understand—

          • Conduct of fire/fire commands.
          • Crew responses.
          • Switchology (smooth and timely application of changes to the ballistic solution

                using the gunner’s lower panel).
          • Fire control system capabilities and function.
          • Battlesight range use and application.
          • Advanced machine gun engagement techniques.
          • Engagements with wide field of view, operations under unusual conditions.
          • Target acquisition with the CITV and designate function.
          • Target acquisition with the CROWS on the offense.

     E-84. The unit’s Master Gunner should leverage the Sabot Academy training materials
     available on these topics and discussions for the tank commander. These are available
     from the Sabot Academy teams site listed in the references at the end of this publication.

     E-85. During scenario development, the Master Gunner must ensure the timing of the
     targetry is synchronized in time and space where the tank does not pass by the second
     set of troops during tactical movement. This requires the Master Gunner to assess the
     size of the maneuver box, the expected firing tank speed, and the range band
     requirement.

                           Synchronize Delay Targets

          On the offense, the Master Gunner must take additional time to
          proof the range based on the expected pace of the firing vehicle.
          Aggressive movement will result in by-passing delay presentations.

     E-106. This engagement requires the crew to establish no-fire zones within the
     CROWS to ensure firing inhibits are in place to prevent damage to the main gun, bore
     evacuator, CITV, or other parts of the tank.
     E-107. This engagement requires exceptional use of the CROWS in the offense which
     will push the crew into uncomfortable territory. It forces proficiency with the set-up
     functions to establish the use of no-fire zones and use of the lead function.

                       ESTABLISHED NO-FIRE ZONES
                                      REQUIRED

                    No crew may execute this task without
                    verification they have set the appropriate no-fire
                    zones for their vehicle. Failure to verify the no-
                    fire zones may result in injury or death to
                    personnel or damaged equipment.

     E-108. The unit’s Master Gunner should leverage the Sabot Academy training
     materials available on these topics and discussions for the tank commander. These are
     available from the Sabot Academy teams site listed in the references at the end of this
     publication.

                      LOADER’S HATCH CLOSED AND
                                        SECURE

                    Range safety officers and the tank commander
                    must verify the loader’s hatch is closed and
                    secure prior to executing this engagement.

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120  467


---

Appendix E

     E-109. The tank commander’s target must be place in the center, between the gunner’s
     targets. This enforces the conditions of the task and reinforces the use of the no-fire
     zones established for the CROWS system during the engagement (see figure E-38).

     Figure E-38. Engagement C9 Master Gunner discussion

468  TC 3-20.31-120  31 July 2025


---

                                                                                    Complex Engagements

E-110. For crews, figure E-39 provides critical notes for the proper execution of this
engagement. The crew must be trained on the capabilities and set-up procedures of the
CROWS to ensure the safety of the crew and live-fire hazards to the tank’s equipment
and armament.

              Figure E-39. Engagement C9 crew discussion

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120                              469

                                      Appendix F

                              Forms

    This appendix is designed for trainers and units to provide clear instructions
    and examples on how to properly fill out all associated forms used where
    this publication is the proponent. Units should download the available forms
    from the Army Publishing Directorate website found in the reference section
    at the back of this manual for downloadable or printable copies of these
    forms.

OVERVIEW OF FORMS

     F-1. This manual prescribes the use of the DA Form 8265-12 series of forms. These
     are used specifically to record basic crew and vehicle information and an official record
     of the screening process including all ballistic information required to track the
     performance of the tank, cannon, fire control system, and ammunition for the unit and
     the Army.
     F-2. Units that fail to complete the forms as intended and submit them to the Maneuver
     Center of Excellence upon completion of screening procedures violates Army policy
     found in AR 350-1. The instructions for each form include methods to submit the
     information to the Maneuver Center of Excellence for data collection and analysis. The
     forms described in this appendix are listed in figure F-1.

                    Figure F-1. Department of the Army forms listing

31 July 2025  TC 3-20.31-120  471


---

Appendix F

DA FORM 8265-12A, CREW PLATFORM DATA
RECORD

     F-3. This one-page form is used to record the vehicle and crew’s basic information. It
     includes the bumper number, vehicle name, model, noun, serial number, national stock
     number and fire control system software version.

     F-4. It continues recording the basic crew information prior to entering a live-fire
     training density. This information includes the following information for each
     crewmember:

          • Name.
          • Experience level.
          • Date assigned to crew.
          • Paragraph and line number from the modified table of organization and

                equipment.
          • Year and month available when known concerning a Soldier’s earliest date of

                departure from the organization.
          • Combat lifesaver training completed.
          • Driver’s license type.

     F-5. The form continues with the tank’s main armament data. This information is
     brought forward from the current DA Form 2408-4, for use by the crew to maintain
     critical information and track fire control system component or armament changes.

     F-6. The tank commander is responsible for maintaining this form for their assigned
     vehicle. This form is an inspectable item as part of the crew packet when entering the
     live-fire training density. It must be completed by the tank commander and submitted to
     the tower prior to entering the firing order during Table IV, screening and zero.

     F-7. Instructions used to complete the form to standard are found in figure F-3 on
     page 475 through figure F-12 on page 484, providing examples of each instruction for
     clarity. A sample DA Form 8265-12A is demonstrated in figure F-2 on pages
     473 and 474.

472  TC 3-20.31-120  31 July 2025

Appendix F

DA FORM 8265-12C, LIVE FIRE ACCURACY
SCREENING TEST (LFAST) WORKSHEET

     F-24. This form is used to record the LFAST firing results for each type of ammunition
     fired. See Figure F-18 for a complete sample DA Form 8265-12C on pages 493 through
     500.

     F-25. This is a multi-page form that includes the following information. It is completed
     by the unit’s Master Gunner. The DA Form 8265-12C includes—

          • Vehicle and crew administrative data.
          • Fire control system status prior to firing, designated as action charlie.
          • Ammunition type round impact locations, designated as action delta, which

                includes—
                 The ability to calculate the MPI for a shot group.
                 Conversion from imperial or metric units to mils.
                 Creation of a discrete CCF.
          • A worksheet that provides a graphic representation for the strike of each round
                fired for the first ammunition type.
          • Proofing team guidance.
          • Fire control system status prior to firing the second ammunition type, termed
                action echo.
          • Second ammunition type round impact locations, termed action foxtrot. This
                includes—
                 The ability to calculate the MPI for a shot group.
                 Conversion from imperial or metric units to mils.
                 Creation of a discrete CCF.
          • A worksheet that provides a graphic representation for the strike of each round
                fired for the second ammunition type.
          • Results overall for each main gun ammunition type screened. The results
                portion—
                 Is used at the beginning of the next gunnery density or maintenance

                     procedure.
                 Includes screening to ensure the appropriate start point fleet or discrete

                     CCF is used.
